# Strata: Testing Document

## 1. Scope and Environment

This document describes how Strata is tested: the Structured Query Language (SQL) terminal and front end, the `flatfile` engine, and the storage and execution layers of the `paged` engine. Every test checks a claim in [DesignDocument.md](DesignDocument.md) or a decision in [DecisionRecord.md](DecisionRecord.md). A row cites the section or the Architecture Decision Record (ADR) number, or states the behavior in one sentence where the design leaves that detail to this document. "Design 2.x" and "Design Document 2.x" both refer to sections of the design document. The prefix of a test ID names the area and the tier:

| Area | Unit | Integration | Environment | Manual |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Front end, Command-Line Interface (CLI) and flatfile engine (Design 2.1 to 2.7, 2.9, 2.14 and 3.1 to 3.7) | `UT-` | `IT-` | `ET-` | `MT-` |
| Paged storage layer, the B+Tree included (Design 2.1, 2.2, 2.6, 2.10 to 2.12, 2.15, 2.17, 3.1, 3.4, 3.8 and 3.10) | `PU-` | `PI-` | `PE-` | None |
| Paged execution layer, the database-level lock, `.engine` and `.dump index` (Design 2.1 to 2.8, 2.13 to 2.16, 3.1 to 3.4, 3.6 to 3.10 and 5.1) | `XU-` | `XI-` | `XE-` | None |
| SQLite differential harness (Design 2.3, 3.6 and 5.1; ADR-0047, ADR-0057), written in Python, not compiled by the CMake build and run by CTest | `DU-` | None | `DE-` | None |
| Generator of the volume data (section 1) | `VU-` | None | None | None |

The matrix covers Design Document 2.1 to 2.17, 3.1 to 3.10 and 5.1, and ADR-0001 to ADR-0057, except for the items below. These have no observable behavior or cannot be triggered reliably by a test.

- Diagram rendering has no automated check. MT-01 inspects the Mermaid diagrams by hand.
- Four statements of 2.17 have no row: the `lsn` field of a node stays untouched by index code, a tree built with small capacities is not reopened with the defaults (the options are not stored), the free list after a failed second `Allocate` of a root split is not compared with its earlier state (PI-30 checks the tree), and a `Rid` that names a page past the end or a page of another table is not injected (PI-38 injects a dead slot).
- ADR-0033 and ADR-0035 name the engine and its types and have no behavior to test. ADR-0055 moves constants and changes no value, so the existing allocator, catalog and database tests are its check.
- Performance and throughput, including the O(n · m) matching time of `LIKE` (2.3): there is no time threshold. The tests check only that the worst case finishes with the correct result, and XE-03 prints the timings of the two engines without asserting them. The fairness and latency of the reader-writer lock depend on timing, so only mutual exclusion and the correctness of results are tested.
- Resource ownership and container cost (2.1, 2.13, 2.16): code review checks them.
- Durability and process termination (2.15), including one in the middle of a paged `ALTER TABLE` or `DROP TABLE`, failures in the rename phase of 3.3, and a failure of the truncation back after a failed `AppendRows` (2.9): a test cannot induce these failures. The same applies to the kernel releasing the ADR-0015 lock after a crash, so ET-18 and ET-32 check only that a second CLI opens a directory after the first one exits normally. The file state left by a process termination in the middle of a paged statement, for example `kill`, is not tested either. No crash consistency guarantee exists until the Write-Ahead Log (WAL) and crash recovery are added, so the paged tests check only that corrupt pages are detected and that errors propagate.
- The `isatty` check and line editing of 3.6: interactive input cannot be reproduced. IT-104 checks the prompt text of `RunRepl` in `is_interactive` mode on string streams, and IT-135 the control-code text of `.clear`.
- Loading does not check `UNIQUE` or foreign keys, so a cascade over duplicate `UNIQUE` values rewrites every referencing row with that value (2.9). XU-24 pins this known, incorrect behavior on the paged engine; flatfile has no such test. The cascade goes only one level deep in both engines (2.5), which has no test.
- An external modification that leaves both size and modification time unchanged goes undetected (2.9). The tests cover only the cases where one of them differs.
- A load worker thread that cannot be started, and a change to a file between its read and its record during a load (2.9): the suite cannot make `pthread_create` fail or place a change inside a load.
- ADR-0011 affects only cost, so IT-15, IT-54 and IT-55 check the result. ET-01 checks the Current state of ADR-0004 (the rejected lines of `students.csv`), VU-06 and XE-01 the rejected lines of `depots.csv` (the shape of the file, and the count of its import), and VU-03 that of ADR-0006 (the distinct `depots.code` and the references of `depot_code`).
- The WAL, crash recovery and transactions do not exist yet, and an index exists only on an INT primary key; a process that ends between the heap write and the index write of a statement is therefore not tested either. The fill factor and the size of an index, and the search time that the layout of ADR-0049 is meant to improve, are measured by hand and asserted by no test. Memory use beyond physical memory, which sorting, hash joins and aggregation can reach, is not tested.
- Two known limitations of 2.15 have no test. One is replacing a paged database whose dirty pages cannot be written out, because a write failure cannot be injected into the real files that `Bootstrap` opens. The other is reopening a directory that was deleted while open.

`CMakeLists.txt` builds `strata_tests` and `strata_cli` and passes the source directory and the path of `strata_cli` to the tests as compile definitions. The differential harness is not compiled by CMake, and `CMakeLists.txt` registers two CTest tests for it when Python 3.10 or later is found, `differential` and `differential_unit` (ADR-0057, DE-04). `python3 -I -m unittest discover -s tests/differential -p 'test_*.py'` runs the `DU-` tests, and with the variable `STRATA_CLI_PATH` set to the built `strata_cli`, which the test property of `differential_unit` does, it also runs the `DE-` tests, which are skipped otherwise; `python3 -I tests/differential/differential.py --cli <strata_cli>` runs the corpus alone. Both need only Python 3.10 or later and its `sqlite3` module. A row that expects no report from AddressSanitizer, UndefinedBehaviorSanitizer or ThreadSanitizer refers to a separate build of the same tests. `CMakeLists.txt` sets `CMAKE_CXX_FLAGS` itself, so such a build sets `CMAKE_CXX_COMPILER` to a wrapper script that adds the `-fsanitize=` option.

The catalog fixture is this `catalog.csv` for the three tables `visitors`, `stories` and `reactions`, in the format of Design Document 2.6, with `visitors.username` declared `UNIQUE`. Fixtures A, B, C1 and C2 all use it.

```text
table,position,column,type,primary_key,unique,auto_increment,has_default,default,ref_table,ref_column
visitors,0,id,INT,1,0,0,0,,,
visitors,1,username,TEXT,0,1,0,0,,,
visitors,2,location,TEXT,0,0,0,0,,,
stories,0,id,INT,1,0,0,0,,,
stories,1,content,TEXT,0,0,0,0,,,
stories,2,username,TEXT,0,0,0,0,,visitors,username
stories,3,views,INT,0,0,0,0,,,
reactions,0,id,INT,1,0,0,0,,,
reactions,1,storyId,INT,0,0,0,0,,stories,id
reactions,2,username,TEXT,0,0,0,0,,visitors,username
reactions,3,type,TEXT,0,0,0,0,,,
reactions,4,comment,TEXT,0,0,0,0,,,
reactions,5,timestamp,INT,0,0,0,0,,,
```

Fixture A is the main data set. Tests write the three Comma-Separated Values (CSV) files below and the catalog to a temporary directory. Lines end in `\n`.

| File | Content (rows after the header) |
|---|---|
| `visitors.csv` (`id,username,location`) | `1,alice,Paris`, `2,carol,Paris`, `3,bob,Rome`, `4,dave,Oslo` |
| `stories.csv` (`id,content,username,views`) | `10,hello world,alice,5`, `11,second story,alice,0`, `12,bobs story,bob,7` |
| `reactions.csv` (`id,storyid,username,type,comment,timestamp`) | `100,10,bob,like,None,1000`, `101,10,carol,comment,nice,1001`, `102,11,bob,comment,ok,1002`, `103,12,alice,like,None,1003`, `104,12,carol,like,None,1004` |

No row references `dave`. `carol` (id 2) is referenced only by `reactions`, while `bob` (id 3) is referenced by `stories` too, so checking in row order and checking in child-table order report different first violations.

Fixture B holds rows that cannot be loaded as written.

| File | Content (rows after the header) | After loading |
|---|---|---|
| `visitors.csv` | `1,alice,Paris`, `x,bad,Nowhere`, `5,eve`, `1,alicia,Rome`, `2,bob,Rome` | 2 rows: `1,alicia,Rome` (the later duplicate id wins) and `2,bob,Rome` |
| `stories.csv` | `10,hello,alice,5`, `11,bad views,bob,abc` | 1 row: `10,hello,alice,5`, which loads although `alice` is not in visitors |
| `reactions.csv` | `100,10,bob,like,None,1`, `101,99,bob,like,None,2`, `102,10,zed,like,None,3`, `103,10,alicia,comment,hi,4` | 4 rows; loading checks no foreign keys, so `storyid` 99 and `zed` are accepted |

In Fixture C1, `visitors.csv` holds `1,a,b` and `2,b,c`, `stories.csv` holds `10,x,a,0` and `11,y,b,0`, and `reactions.csv` has only the header. Fixture C2 differs only in `visitors.csv`, which holds `1,a,b` and `2,b,a`.

Fixture D covers quoting. Its catalog is one table, `notes(id INT PRIMARY KEY, body TEXT)`, and the rows of `notes.csv` are `1,"a,b"`, `2,"say ""hi"""`, `3,plain`, `4,""`, `5,"unterminated`, `6,"ok"x`.

Unit tests take their schema from `FakeCatalog`, a fake `ICatalog` with the three tables of the catalog fixture, `measures(id INT, score DOUBLE)`, `drafts(id INT, body TEXT, owner TEXT DEFAULT 'none', score DOUBLE DEFAULT 1.0)` and `jobs(id INT, body TEXT)` with an auto-increment `id`. A decorator around `IFlatFileRepository` records each `Commit` call with the names of the tables it writes (`written_table_names`) and counts the `AppendRows` calls (`append_calls`) and can make either call return a failure description.

`sample_my_db/` is a small course enrollment database: `instructors` (6 rows), `students` (30), `courses` (12) and `enrollments` (144), plus `catalog.csv`. The README examples use it. ET-33 to ET-35 work on a temporary copy of it as it is, ET-16, ET-19, ET-32 and ET-36 use no copy, and the other CLI Environment tests work on a temporary copy whose `students.csv` has three added lines that the schema rejects (section 3.3).

The volume tests use generated data (`tests/test_scale_data_support.cpp`) and no file in the repository. The data is three tables of 10000 rows each that track parcels through depots: `depots(id INT, code TEXT, town TEXT)`, `parcels(id INT, label TEXT, depot_code TEXT, weight_g INT)` and `scans(id INT, parcel_id INT, depot_code TEXT, status TEXT, remark TEXT, scanned_at INT)`. The words come from fixed lists and every choice from a fixed pseudo-random sequence (SplitMix64), so every run produces the same rows. Depot 1 has the town `Wrenfold`, which no other depot has, and the six scans that the generator plants for depot 1 (three `received`, two `delayed`, one with an empty status) give the queries about that town a fixed answer. `WriteScaleCsv` writes the tables as CSV files with `\r\n` line ends and puts three lines that the schema rejects into `depots.csv`: line 12 has `x10` where the id belongs, line 17 has 2 fields and line 18 has 4. The values follow these rules: the depot codes are distinct, every `depot_code` of `parcels` and `scans` is a depot code, `parcels` never uses the code of depot 1, and `scans` uses it only in the six planted probe scans; `weight_g` is 100 to 20099, `parcel_id` is 1 to 10000, and `scanned_at` is 1735689600 (2025-01-01 UTC) to 1767225599 (2025-12-31 UTC); no text contains a comma, a double quote, `\r` or `\n`; a label has 13 to 119 characters, starts with a capital letter and ends with a period; a `delayed` scan has a remark of 11 to 68 characters and every other remark is `-`; about 54, 36 and 10 percent of the scans are `received`, `delayed` and empty.

The directory permission tests make a directory unwritable, and `TempDir` restores owner permissions before it removes the directory. Permissions have no effect for the root user, so `test_directory_lock.cpp` and the other tests that need an unwritable directory end with `SKIP` there and the output marks them; `test_paged_database_reports_directory_problems` (PI-06) and `CheckUnavailableDirectoryIsRefused` (XI-12) skip only their permission case inside the test and report `[  OK  ]` for the rest.

The paged tests use only temporary directories and the in-memory `MemoryPageStore`, except for the Environment tests and the layering checks XU-32 and PI-12, which read the sources. The Environment tests build the volume data in memory or write it to a temporary directory, and XE-02 reads `sample_my_db/`, which is inside the repository. XE-02 is skipped when `sample_my_db/` is missing; no other test is skipped for a missing file. This document does not record the date, environment or result of a test run.

## 2. Test Strategy

### 2.1 Tiers

Unit tests replace every dependency of the component under test with a fake. Integration tests run real components across layers, over temporary directories or the in-memory `MemoryPageStore`. Environment tests use the generated volume data and `sample_my_db/`, and the flatfile ones run the built `strata_cli` executable.

| Tier | Boundary Under Test | Real Components | Replaced by Fake or Mock |
|---|---|---|---|
| Unit | Pure logic of Domain and Adapter (statement splitting, the `.schema` text); pure functions of Infrastructure (CSV and catalog encoding); Application orchestration | `SqlParser`, `Binder`, expression evaluation, `CsvCodec`, `StatementBuffer`, `FormatCreateTable`, `FormatParseError`, `InferImport`, `QueryService` | `FakeCatalog`; `IEngine` in the `QueryService` tests |
| Unit, paged storage | The byte layer (`crc32c`, `byte_codec`, `TupleCodec`, `SlottedPage`, the B+Tree node functions and `CheckTree`), the replacement policies, and the boundary between `IPageStore` and `BufferManager`, `TableHeap`, `Catalog` and `BTree` | The classes under test and the pure functions beneath them | `MemoryPageStore` stands in for `IPageStore`, and read, write, allocate and sync failures are injected through it; a map of hand-built pages stands in for `INodeSource` |
| Unit, paged execution | The row layer (`operators`, `row_keys`, `key_range`, `mutation_planner`), `ToExecutionError`, the lock of `QueryService` and the dependency direction | The pure functions and classes under test | `IRowStream` and `ITableReader` are implemented over an in-memory `std::vector`, with a version that counts calls, and `IIndexReader` by a fake that records its calls. `ICatalog` is replaced by `MapCatalog`, and the lock tests wrap `FakeCatalog`. `IEngine` is replaced by a fake that records the number of concurrent executions |
| Unit, volume data | The generator of the volume data: its shape, determinism, planted values, text rules and CSV files | `GenerateScaleTables`, `ScaleTables`, `WriteScaleCsv` | None |
| Integration | Across layers: `SqlParser`, `Binder`, `FlatFileEngine`, `CsvRepository`, `DirectoryLock`, `Bootstrap`, `RunRepl` (string streams stand in for stdin and stdout), `.import` through `QueryService` and `FileCsvSource`, and the file system | All layers under test, with the database directory in a temporary directory | `IFlatFileRepository`, through a decorator, in the write-failure tests |
| Integration, paged storage | The database file: `FilePageStore`, `Database`, file format validation, multiple threads, replacement policy switching, dependency direction | Real files in a temporary directory, and the complete paged layer | None, except that one sync failure case goes through `MemoryPageStore`. Corrupt pages, truncation and a version mismatch are produced by rewriting file bytes |
| Integration, paged execution | `PagedEngine` plus `Database`, `QueryService` plus the two engines, `Bootstrap`, the `.engine` command and the equivalence of the two engines | The real `PagedEngine`, `Database`, `BufferManager`, `QueryService`, `FlatFileEngine`, `CsvRepository` and `Cli` | `IPageStore` is replaced by `MemoryPageStore`, into which failures and byte rewrites can be injected. The durability tests use real files in a temporary directory. On the flatfile side of the equivalence tests the real `CsvRepository` runs over a temporary directory, so there is no in-memory repository fake |
| Environment | The `strata_cli` executable on copies of `sample_my_db/`; the dependency direction of the source code | Complete system | None |
| Environment, paged | The generated volume data and `sample_my_db/`, with a buffer pool smaller than the data | The complete paged layer, both engines and `FileCsvSource` | None |
| Unit, differential | The comparison, the parsing of the CLI output and of the corpus in the Python harness | The harness functions | `strata_cli`, replaced by canned CLI text, and SQLite, replaced by canned values except in DU-03 |
| Environment, differential | The built `strata_cli` with each engine against the `sqlite3` module, one process per statement | Complete system | None |

### 2.2 Partitions and Coverage

| Input | Technique | Partition or Boundary | Covered By |
|---|---|---|---|
| Statement kind | Equivalence Partitioning | `SELECT`, `INSERT`, `UPDATE`, `DELETE`, `CREATE TABLE`, `ALTER TABLE`, `DROP TABLE`, out-of-scope syntax | UT-01, UT-02, UT-08, UT-09, UT-10, UT-15, UT-16, UT-17, UT-58, UT-60, UT-61 |
| Expression type combination | Equivalence Partitioning | INT/INT, INT/DOUBLE, TEXT/TEXT, TEXT/numeric | UT-20, UT-21, UT-22, UT-34 |
| Integer literal and result | Boundary Value Analysis | 0; `int64_t` maximum, minimum, maximum plus 1; overflowing result; value past 32 bits | UT-06, UT-07, UT-12, UT-26, IT-18, IT-25, IT-42, IT-70, IT-86 |
| Input row count of an aggregate | Boundary Value Analysis | 0, 1, several rows | UT-24, UT-25, IT-13, IT-17 |
| Number of tables in `FROM` | Boundary Value Analysis | 1 to 4 tables | IT-11, IT-12, IT-14, IT-116, IT-117, UT-46 |
| Key of `ORDER BY` | Boundary Value Analysis | alias; position 0, 1, last, last plus 1; expression equal to a select item; key not in the list; alias named like a column | UT-41 |
| Values of `INSERT` | Equivalence Partitioning | valid; missing foreign key target; duplicate primary key or `UNIQUE`; TEXT with newline, comma or quotes; type mismatch; missing or duplicate column; omitted column (`DEFAULT`, auto-increment, neither) | IT-20 to IT-26, IT-87, IT-88, IT-90, UT-42, UT-78 to UT-80 |
| Target column of `UPDATE` | Equivalence Partitioning | ordinary, referenced, foreign key, primary key or `UNIQUE` column | IT-29 to IT-35, IT-89, UT-43 |
| Row count and value of `UPDATE` | Boundary Value Analysis | 0, 1, several rows; no `WHERE`; new value equal to the old | IT-34, IT-35, IT-36 |
| Reference state of `DELETE` | Equivalence Partitioning | unreferenced; referenced by stories or reactions; some of several rows referenced; no `WHERE`; 0 rows | IT-36 to IT-40 |
| Validity of a CSV row | Equivalence Partitioning | valid; non-numeric integer; too few columns; duplicate primary key, also `-0.0` after `0.0`; missing foreign key target (unchecked at load); unbalanced quotes | IT-01, IT-43, IT-67, IT-68, IT-152 |
| Quoting of a CSV column | Equivalence Partitioning | none; comma; double quote; empty string; empty string as the only column; unbalanced quotes | UT-65 to UT-69, UT-133, IT-68, IT-75, IT-90, IT-149 |
| Catalog file | Equivalence Partitioning | valid; missing; malformed | UT-71, UT-72, IT-10, IT-63, IT-64, IT-85 |
| CSV of a table | Equivalence Partitioning | matching header; missing; header mismatch; 0 bytes; header only; not in the catalog | IT-03, IT-65, IT-66, IT-69 |
| File state | Equivalence Partitioning | all open; missing; directory not writable; empty file; leftover `.orig` at a commit, an append or a load; reserved or duplicate name; not a regular file; symbolic link; read-only file; an append cut by the file size limit | IT-02, IT-03, IT-07, IT-08, IT-41, IT-44, IT-47 to IT-53, IT-60, IT-153, IT-154, ET-14 |
| Newline format | Equivalence Partitioning | `\n`; `\r\n`; no final newline; blank `\r\n` line before the header | IT-05, IT-06, IT-148 |
| Identifier case | Equivalence Partitioning | declared; lowercase; uppercase | UT-04, UT-47, IT-19 |
| Data Definition Language (DDL) input | Equivalence Partitioning | valid; table exists; reserved name; duplicate column; zero or several primary keys; auto-increment type; non-constant or mistyped `DEFAULT`; invalid foreign key target; added column without `DEFAULT` or with restrictions; TEXT `DEFAULT` with a line break | UT-58 to UT-64, UT-73 to UT-77, IT-145 |
| Adopting an existing CSV | Equivalence Partitioning | missing file; matching file; duplicate primary key; duplicate `UNIQUE`; orphan foreign key; header mismatch; not a regular file; 0 bytes | IT-76 to IT-80, ET-18 |
| Auto-increment | Boundary Value Analysis | empty table; table with rows; explicit value; after deleting the maximum; `int64_t` maximum | UT-79, IT-86 |
| Directory lock | State Transition | not held; held; after release; unwritable directory; reopen in the same process; reopen after the directory became writable; open failure | IT-91, IT-92, IT-94 to IT-96, IT-151, ET-21, ET-22 |
| Atomicity of DDL | Fault Injection | temporary file failure; `Commit` failure | IT-74, IT-84 |
| Free list of a paged file | State Transition | empty; one released chain; two chains; after a pop; after reuse; head past the file, a repeated page, a page of the wrong type, a chain that is not a heap chain, a successor below page 3 | PU-49 to PU-53, PU-60, PI-15, PI-19 |
| Page chain of a table that `ADD COLUMN` rewrites | Boundary Value Analysis | empty table; one page; many pages; a row that just fits and one that does not with the added column; a default that fits in a catalog row and one that does not | PI-16, PI-17, XI-26 |
| Position of a failure during paged `ALTER TABLE` or `DROP TABLE` | Fault Injection | the n-th read, write and allocation, for n from 1 until the statement succeeds (`DROP TABLE` allocates no page, so its allocation sweep never fails); a read and a write failure in one statement | PI-18, PI-20, PI-24, XI-28 |
| Reference state of a paged `DROP TABLE` | Equivalence Partitioning | unreferenced table; table that another table references; table dropped and created again under the same name | PI-19, XI-27 |
| Statement of the differential corpus | Equivalence Partitioning | query, aggregate, join, sort, function, DDL, write, evaluation error, bind error, constraint error; agreeing and marked as differing | DE-01 (the statement kinds of the corpus), DU-06 and DE-02 (agreeing and marked as differing) |
| Line of statement input | Equivalence Partitioning | one or several statements per line; several lines; `;` in a literal; literal across lines; empty statement; empty line; incomplete at end | UT-82 to UT-85, IT-98, IT-99, ET-24 |
| Command | Equivalence Partitioning | valid; extra argument; statement in progress; not connected; table missing | IT-100 to IT-108, ET-25, ET-27 |
| Prompt | Equivalence Partitioning | interactive; pipe | IT-104, ET-26 |
| Column type for import | Boundary Value Analysis | canonical and non-canonical INT (`007`, `+5`, `-0`) and DOUBLE (`1.5`, `1.50`, `10`, a subnormal value); blank cell; no data rows; `--type` override | UT-125, UT-129, UT-131, UT-137 |
| Bad row in import | Equivalence Partitioning | wrong column count; malformed quotes; mismatch with `--type`; duplicate primary key, also `-0.0` after `0.0`; strict mode and `--skip-bad-rows` | UT-128, UT-130, UT-138, IT-132, IT-133, ET-38 |
| Name and primary key for import | Equivalence Partitioning | valid, invalid, reserved or duplicate column name; invalid, reserved or existing table name; `--pk` existing or unknown column; default `id` | UT-126, UT-127, UT-134 |
| Target file of import | Equivalence Partitioning | missing; regular file not in the catalog; the source file itself; a directory | IT-146, IT-147 |
| Opening a database directory | Equivalence Partitioning | exists; missing with existing parent; parent missing; path is a file; creation fails | IT-93, IT-125, IT-126, IT-141, ET-35 |
| External modification | State Transition | unchanged; changed (size and time differ); only size differs; only time differs; own consecutive writes; reopen; file adopted by `CREATE TABLE` | IT-129, IT-130, IT-144, IT-150 |
| Input tolerance | Equivalence Partitioning | comment; whitespace outside American Standard Code for Information Interchange (ASCII); full-width semicolon; command name without `.`; `.cancel` in Idle, Pending and InString; prompt inside a string | UT-118 to UT-124, IT-135 to IT-140 |
| Position of a syntax error | Boundary Value Analysis | single line; first line; later lines; end of input | UT-86 |
| Operator precedence | Equivalence Partitioning | `OR`; `AND`; comparison and `LIKE`; `+ -`; `* /`; `\|\|`; negative sign | UT-03, UT-89, UT-91 |
| Arithmetic value | Boundary Value Analysis | INT and DOUBLE; sign of integer division; divisor 0 (INT, `0.0`, `-0.0`); minimum divided by `-1`; multiplication overflow | UT-109, IT-114, IT-115 |
| `LIKE` pattern | Boundary Value Analysis | no wildcard; `%`; `_`; empty text; empty pattern; consecutive `%`; case; non-ASCII; invalid bytes; backtracking | UT-111, UT-112, IT-112 |
| Argument of a string function | Boundary Value Analysis | empty string; ASCII; multibyte character; invalid bytes; `SUBSTR` start of 0, negative or past the end; length 0 or negative; start plus length overflow | UT-113, UT-114, IT-113 |
| Input of `ROUND` and `CAST` | Boundary Value Analysis | exactly half; just below half; negative; `digits` of 0, 15, 16, -1; valid and invalid TEXT-to-numeric forms; edge of the INT range; a subnormal DOUBLE text or literal, and one that would round to 0 | UT-115, UT-116, UT-135, UT-136, IT-114 |
| Argument count and type of a function | Equivalence Partitioning | too few; too many; type mismatch; `kBool` | UT-102, UT-107 |
| Aggregate function and input row count | Boundary Value Analysis | 0 rows for `AVG`, `MIN`, `MAX` (with and without `GROUP BY`); 1 row; several rows; INT, DOUBLE, TEXT | UT-103, UT-117, IT-110, IT-111 |
| Value of `LIMIT` and `OFFSET` | Boundary Value Analysis | 0; below and above the row count; `OFFSET` past the row count; `int64_t` maximum; negative; decimal; non-literal | UT-95, UT-96, IT-109, ET-28 |
| Rows of a multi-row `INSERT` | Equivalence Partitioning | all valid; a later row fails a check; duplicate primary key or `UNIQUE` between rows; missing foreign key target; auto-increment overflow; write failure | UT-97, UT-106, IT-119 to IT-123, ET-32 |
| CRC input | Boundary Value Analysis | Empty input, standard vectors, lengths 0 to 300, a single bit flip in each of the 4096 bytes | PU-01, PU-02, PU-16 |
| Integer byte order | Boundary Value Analysis | 0, 1, the maximum value, byte order | PU-03 |
| Tuple column values | Boundary Value Analysis | INT extreme values, DOUBLE `-0.0`, NaN, infinity, subnormal numbers, TEXT that is empty, long or contains special bytes | PU-04 |
| Tuple encoding and decoding errors | Boundary Value Analysis, Error Guessing | Column count and type mismatch, total length 4060 and 4061, too few bytes, extra bytes, length overrun | PU-05, PU-06 |

## 3. Test Matrix

### 3.1 Unit

The unit tier runs in memory, with a fake engine and a `FakeCatalog` in place of the real ones. It covers the parser, the Binder, expression and aggregate evaluation, the CSV and catalog codecs, the statement buffer, error and schema formatting, import inference and `QueryService`.

| Test ID | Claim | Technique | Procedure | Expected Result | Not Covered |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| UT-01 | Design 2.3 statement `SELECT`; ADR-0003 | Equivalence Partitioning | Parses `SELECT * FROM visitors`; `test_parser_select_star_builds_ast` | One star item, `from.table` `visitors` without alias, no joins, null `where` | Semantic checks (Binder) |
| UT-02 | Design 2.3 statement `SELECT`; ADR-0010 | Equivalence Partitioning | Parses `SELECT v.username AS name, COUNT(*) FROM visitors AS v JOIN stories s ON v.username = s.username WHERE v.id > 1 GROUP BY v.username ORDER BY name DESC` and `SELECT id FROM visitors ORDER BY id ASC`; `test_parser_select_full_clauses_builds_ast` | First: two items (the first aliased `name`), `from.alias` `v`, one join aliased `s`, and `where`, `group_by`, `order_by` set with `is_descending` true. Second: `is_descending` false | Semantic checks |
| UT-03 | Design 2.3 operators | Equivalence Partitioning | Parses `a OR b AND c`, `1 - 2 - 3`, `a = 1 + 2`; `test_parser_expression_precedence_orders_operators` | `OR(a, AND(b, c))`, `SUB(SUB(1, 2), 3)`, `EQ(a, ADD(1, 2))` | Parentheses (see UT-18) |
| UT-04 | Design 2.3 identifiers | Equivalence Partitioning | Parses `select ID from VISITORS where Id = 1`; `test_parser_keywords_are_case_insensitive` | A `SelectStatement` whose `from.table` keeps the spelling `VISITORS` | Identifier binding (see UT-47) |
| UT-05 | Design 2.3 string literals | Boundary Value Analysis | Parses the literals `'it''s'` and `''`; `test_parser_string_literal_handles_doubled_quote` | TEXT `it's`, then the empty string | A literal containing a newline |
| UT-06 | Design 2.3 numeric literals | Boundary Value Analysis | Parses `0`, `3.14`, `9223372036854775807`; `test_parser_numeric_literals_keep_type` | INT 0, DOUBLE 3.14, INT 9223372036854775807 | Exponent notation (out of scope, see UT-12) |
| UT-07 | A `-` directly before a numeric literal at an operand position is a negative literal, `-9223372036854775808` included, a `-` at an operator position subtracts, and any other `-` parses as `0 - <operand>` | Boundary Value Analysis | Parses `-5`, `-9223372036854775808`, `-3.5`, `-views`, `1 - -2`, `1-2`; `test_parser_negative_literals_and_lowering` | INT -5, INT minimum, DOUBLE -3.5, `SUB(0, views)`, `SUB(1, -2)`, `SUB(1, 2)` | None |

| UT-10 | Design 2.3 statement `DELETE` | Equivalence Partitioning | Parses a `DELETE` with and without `WHERE`; `test_parser_delete_optional_where` | `where` set, then null | None |

The paged storage unit tests (PU) cover the byte layer, the replacement policies and the classes above `IPageStore`, which run over `MemoryPageStore`.

| Test ID | Claim | Technique | Procedure | Expected Result | Not Covered |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|

The differential unit tests (DU) are Python `unittest` cases in `tests/differential/test_compare.py`. They feed the comparison and the parsing functions canned values and canned text, and run no `strata_cli`; only DU-03 opens an in-memory SQLite database, to get the exceptions that SQLite really raises.

| Test ID | Claim | Technique | Procedure | Expected Result | Not Covered |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| DU-01 | ADR-0047 (comparison by the SQLite type); Design 2.3 (DOUBLE text) | Equivalence Partitioning, Boundary Value Analysis | Compare a Strata cell with a SQLite value for an INT, a DOUBLE and a TEXT: equal; `1` against SQLite `1.0`; `1.0000000001` and `1.1` against `1.0`; `-0.0` against `0.0`; the TEXT `-0.0` against the TEXT `0.0`; a TEXT that looks like a number; `test_compare_reads_cells_by_the_sqlite_type` | INT and TEXT must match exactly. A DOUBLE matches within a relative 1e-9 or an absolute 1e-12, so `-0.0` matches `0.0` as numbers but the two TEXT values do not match. A cell whose type the SQLite value contradicts, such as `1` for a REAL, is a mismatch | A DOUBLE above 2^53 |
| DU-02 | ADR-0047 (rows as a multiset or a sequence) | Equivalence Partitioning | Compare two row lists in another order, with and without the total-order marker, and with a different row count or a repeated row; `test_compare_rows_as_multiset_or_sequence` | Another order matches without the marker and does not match with it. A different count, or a row repeated a different number of times, never matches | A partial order, which the corpus does not use |
| DU-03 | ADR-0047 (errors compare by class); Design 3.7 | Equivalence Partitioning | Map `error: constraint violation: ...`, the other four `error: <kind>: ...` lines, a `syntax error at column ...` line, and the `sqlite3` exceptions `IntegrityError`, `OperationalError` and `ProgrammingError`; `test_compare_error_classes` | A constraint violation and an `IntegrityError` are the class `constraint`. The other errors and the other exceptions are the class `other`. Two errors of one class match, and of two classes do not | The messages, which are not compared |
| DU-04 | ADR-0047 (one process per statement); Design 3.6 output forms | Equivalence Partitioning | Parse canned CLI text: the lines before the marker query, a result set with Tab-separated rows, `OK affected=<n>`, `OK <tag>` with `note:` lines, and an empty result set; `test_cli_output_is_split_at_the_marker` | The lines up to and including the marker result are dropped. A result set gives its column names and rows, `OK affected=<n>` gives a count, and `OK <tag>` and an empty list give the outcome `done`, without comparing the tag. A text without the marker is an error of the harness | The prompt text, which a pipe does not print |
| DU-05 | ADR-0047 (corpus format) | Equivalence Partitioning | Parse a corpus text with a `;` inside a string literal, a `--` comment, a `-- differs: <area>` marker before a statement, a `-- order: total` marker, two statements on one line and a last statement without `;`; `test_corpus_parsing` | The statements are split outside string literals, each with its markers, and a marker applies to the next statement only. An unknown marker is an error of the harness | None |
| DU-06 | ADR-0047 (marker enforcement) | State Transition | Run the comparison over canned results for each case: agreement, unmarked difference, marked difference, marked statement that agrees, and a difference between the two engines; `test_marker_enforcement` | Agreement is `agree` and a marked difference is `expected`. The other three cases are `unexpected`: the detail of an unmarked difference says what Strata and SQLite answered, that of a marked statement that agrees names the area, and that of two engines that disagree shows what each printed. `Report.summary()` prints the three counts | The statement text in a report and the counting in `run_corpus`, which DE-01 and DE-02 check |
| DU-07 | ADR-0047 (the harness runs `strata_cli` once per statement) | Boundary Value Analysis | Run `run_strata` with a shell script as the CLI that prints the marker line and then exits with status 1, as a crash after the marker would; `test_run_strata_refuses_a_nonzero_exit_status` | The harness raises `HarnessError`, so an empty result after the marker is `done` only when the exit status is 0 | A CLI that crashes before the marker (the missing marker is already an error), and how the harness reports a signal |

The paged execution unit tests (XU) cover the row layer over in-memory row streams, `ToExecutionError`, the lock of `QueryService` with a fake engine, and the dependency direction.

| Test ID | Claim | Technique | Procedure | Expected Result | Not Covered |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| XU-01 | `BuildOperatorTree` turns a `ScanNode` into an operator that yields the rows of the stream, and returns the error of `ITableReader::Scan` unchanged for a table it cannot scan, also below another node | Equivalence Partitioning | Build an operator tree for `ScanNode` over a vector reader and pull all rows. Repeat for the same table spelled in mixed case, for a table that does not exist, and for a missing table below a `SortNode`; `test_operators_scan_yields_stream_rows_and_unknown_table` | The rows and columns match the order and content of the stream. The error of the reader is returned unchanged: with the vector reader a mixed-case name finds the table, and a table that does not exist gives `kBind` with the message `unknown table: <name>` as spelled, and the same message when the scan sits below another operator | The real page order (XI-01); the real `DatabaseTableReader` (XI-07) |
| XU-02 | Design Document 2.13 `FilterNode` and `ProjectNode` | Equivalence Partitioning, Error Guessing | Build Filter plus Project over a vector stream for `SELECT id, 100 / x FROM t WHERE y > 0`, with rows that pass and rows that do not. Run `SELECT 100 / x` where `x` is 0 in the third row, pulling one `Next` at a time. Put the same division in a `WHERE` clause; `test_operators_filter_project_and_runtime_errors` | Only the rows that pass are returned, the projected values are correct, and `Columns()` is `output_columns`. On failure `kRuntime` is returned with the message `division by zero`, in the projection and in the filter alike, after the rows that precede the failing row. A `Next` call after the error returns no value, and the stream is not called again | `Evaluate` failures other than division by zero, which the UT expression and function rows cover |
| XU-03 | Design Document 2.13 `JoinNode` (hash key, output order) | Equivalence Partitioning | Join two vector streams on `l.a = r.a`, where right has duplicate keys and left has rows without a match; `test_operators_hash_join_matches_and_order` | The output follows the left order, and the matches of one left row follow the right order. Left rows without a match are not output. `Columns()` is left followed by right | Non-equality conditions (XU-05). A join whose right side is empty: XI-18 runs one without an error on the left side, but nothing asserts that the left side is still drained |
| XU-04 | Design Document 2.13 "hash key"; 2.13 `JoinNode` (`ValuesEqual` confirmation) | Boundary Value Analysis | Join an INT column with a DOUBLE column, `2` against `2.0`, with either side on the left. Join the two INT values `9007199254740992` and `9007199254740993`. Join TEXT keys that differ in a trailing space or in case; `test_operators_hash_join_cross_type_and_large_int_keys` | `2` and `2.0` match. The two INT values 2^53 and 2^53 + 1 that share a double key do not match. TEXT keys match byte for byte | Other type combinations. The Binder already rejects incompatible comparisons |
| XU-05 | Design Document 2.13 `JoinNode` (residual condition, nested loop) | Equivalence Partitioning | Run the conditions `l.a = r.a AND l.b < r.b`, `l.a < r.a` with no equality, `r.a = l.a` with the operands in reverse order, and two equality conjuncts. Add a residual `100 / (r.b - 3) > 0` that divides by zero for one right row; `test_operators_join_residual_and_nested_loop` | The result equals the set computed by hand with nested loops. The output of the nested loop follows the left order. The failing residual returns `kRuntime` with the message `division by zero` | A `JoinNode` without an `ON` condition: the parser requires `ON`, so only a hand-built plan has none, and no test builds one. Performance |

The volume data unit tests (VU) are in `tests/test_scale_data.cpp`. They check the generator of the volume data (section 1) without the engines.

| Test ID | Claim | Technique | Procedure | Expected Result | Not Covered |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|

| VU-02 | Section 1, the same rows on every run | Equivalence Partitioning | Call `GenerateScaleTables` twice and compare both results with `ScaleTables()`; `test_scale_data_generation_is_deterministic` | The names and the rows of all three are equal | Another platform or standard library, which the fingerprint of VU-06 covers |
| VU-03 | Section 1, keys, references and value ranges | Boundary Value Analysis | Collect the codes and towns of `depots`, then check every row of `parcels` and `scans`; `test_scale_data_keys_and_references_are_valid` | The 10000 codes are distinct, and the towns are more than 3000 and fewer than 10000 distinct values. Every `depot_code` of `parcels` and `scans` is a depot code, and `parcels` never uses the code of depot 1. `weight_g` lies in 100 to 20099, `parcel_id` in 1 to 10000 and `scanned_at` in 1735689600 to 1767225599 | Foreign keys are not declared in the schemas, so no engine enforces them here |
| VU-04 | Section 1, the planted values | Equivalence Partitioning | Count the depots whose town is `kScaleProbeTown`, collect the scans of the code of depot 1, and count the codes that start with `D000`; `test_scale_data_plants_the_probe_values` | Only depot 1 has the town `Wrenfold`. The scans of depot 1 are scans 1 to 6, with the statuses `received` three times, `delayed` twice and the empty string once. 99 codes start with `D000` | None |
| VU-05 | Section 1, the text of the volume data | Boundary Value Analysis | Check every text value, the label of every parcel, and the status and remark of every scan; `test_scale_data_text_follows_its_rules` | No text contains `,`, `"`, `\r` or `\n`. Line 12 of `depots.csv` starts with `x10,` and has 3 fields, line 17 starts with `15,` and has 2 fields, line 18 starts with `15,` and has 4, and line 19 is row 15 with 3. Exactly 3 lines of `depots.csv` are rejected, the lines of `parcels.csv` have 4 fields and those of `scans.csv` 6. The FNV-1a fingerprint of the three files equals the pinned value 17521446498847180916 | Reading the files with the engine, which XE-01 does |

### 3.2 Integration

Integration tests run the parser, binder, `FlatFileEngine`, `CsvRepository`, `DirectoryLock`, `Bootstrap` and `RunRepl` together on real directories under a temporary path. Fixtures A, B, C1, C2 and D and the decorator repository are described in section 1.

| Test ID | Claim | Technique | Procedure | Expected Result | Not Covered |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| IT-01 | Design 2.9 load-time rules; ADR-0004 | Equivalence Partitioning | Load Fixture B; `test_csv_load_rejects_invalid_rows` | Matches the "after load" column of Fixture B: 2 visitors, 1 story, 4 reactions. No foreign key check runs at load, so the story whose `stories.username` is not in visitors still loads | Load speed |
| IT-02 | Design 3.1 steps 5 and 6 | Equivalence Partitioning | Load Fixtures A and B with `Load(false)` and with `Load(true)`; `test_csv_load_parallel_equals_serial` | The serial and parallel tables are equal row by row | Thread interleavings |
| IT-03 | Design 3.1 steps 5, 6, 12 and 13; Design 2.9 load-time rules | Equivalence Partitioning | Load three directories that lack `visitors.csv`, `stories.csv` and `reactions.csv` in turn; `test_csv_load_missing_table_file_returns_description` | `cannot read visitors.csv`, `cannot read stories.csv`, `cannot read reactions.csv`, in that order | A file without read permission |

The paged storage integration tests (PI) use real files in a temporary directory and the complete paged layer, except PI-11, which uses `MemoryPageStore`, and PI-12, which scans the source files.

| Test ID | Claim | Technique | Procedure | Expected Result | Not Covered |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| PI-01 | Design 2.10 new database; 3.1 open steps 3 and 4; ADR-0024 | Equivalence Partitioning | Run `Database::Open` on an empty temporary directory, `Close` it and read `paged.db` as raw bytes. Separately call `Open` on an existing 0-byte `paged.db`; `test_paged_database_creates_a_new_file` | The file size is 12288. In page 0, `magic` is `PAGEDDB` plus `0x00`, `format_version` is 2, `page_size` is 4096, `next_table_id` is 3, and the first pages of the two system tables are 1 and 2. The `page_type` of page 0 is 1, and `lsn` and `reserved` are 0. The CRC and `page_id` of the three pages are correct, and `TableNames` is empty. The 0-byte file opens successfully and becomes a new database of 12288 bytes | None |
| PI-02 | Design 2.10 file header, 2.15 file size and version; 3.1 open step 5 | Error Guessing | Corrupt a copy of a valid file one item at a time and `Open` each copy. The items are one extra byte of value 1 at the end, truncation to 2 pages, one byte of `magic`, `format_version` set to 3, `page_size` set to 8192 (CRC sealed again), one byte of the header (CRC mismatch), `page_id` of the header set to 1 (sealed again), `sys_tables_first_page` beyond the page count (sealed again), `sys_columns_first_page` beyond the page count (sealed again), `page_type` of page 0 set to 2 (sealed again), and `next_table_id` set to 2 (sealed again); `test_paged_database_rejects_invalid_headers` | Every item is `kCorrupt`, with a message naming the mismatched item: size, `magic`, `format_version`, `page_size`, checksum, `page_id`, `sys_tables_first_page`, `sys_columns_first_page`, `page_type` or `next_table_id`. The bytes of the file are the same before and after `Open` | Other truncation lengths greater than 0 and less than 3 pages, because only 2 pages are tested |
| PI-03 | Design 3.1; 2.6; 2.11 | Equivalence Partitioning | Create three tables, insert 500 rows into each and `Close`. `Open` again with the original pool and policy and read the rows back. Then `Open` once more with 4 frames and 2Q and read back again; `test_paged_database_reopen_preserves_catalog_and_rows` | After both reopens the catalog equals the catalog at creation, the rows of each table, sorted by `id`, equal the inserted rows, and the free-space map equals the one before closing | The case where `Close` is not called before the database is closed; see PI-10 |
| PI-04 | Design 2.15 damaged data after open; 2.11 opening a table | Fault Injection | After `Close`, rewrite one byte of one heap page of table B in the file, `Open` and call `Table` for A and B. In a second case rewrite one byte of the first page of `sys_tables` and `Open`; `test_paged_database_isolates_corrupt_table_pages` | First case: `Open` succeeds, `Table(A)` succeeds and scans correctly, and `Table(B)` is `kCorrupt` with a message containing the page id. Second case: `Open` is `kCorrupt` | None |
| PI-05 | Design 2.15 file size | Boundary Value Analysis | Append one all-zero page of 4096 bytes to the end of a valid file and `Open` it. Create a table, insert rows, `Close` and `Open` again; `test_paged_database_accepts_trailing_unformatted_page` | `Open` succeeds, and the inserted rows exist after the reopen | Whether the extra page stays unused |
| PI-06 | Design 3.1 open steps 1 and 2; 2.15 | Equivalence Partitioning | Call `Open` on a directory that does not exist, on the path of a regular file, and on a directory that is not writable. The last case is skipped when the effective user is root; `test_paged_database_reports_directory_problems` | All three are `kIo`, the first with a message, and no file is created in the non-writable directory | The message texts |
| PI-07 | Design 2.11 concurrency contract; 2.12; ADR-0028 | Equivalence Partitioning | Use three tables of about 3000 rows each, about 100 pages or more, and 16 frames. Call `Table` on the three tables, then `ResetStats`. Then run 8 threads that each do a full `Scan` of the three tables 5 times and compute the row count and an order-independent hash. Do this once per policy, and run the same test under ThreadSanitizer (TSan); `test_paged_database_concurrent_scans_with_small_pool` | The row count and hash of every thread equal the expected values, and no `kNoFrame` occurs. `hits + misses` equals `8 * 5 * the total page count of the three tables`. `PinnedFrameCount` is 0 at the end, and TSan reports nothing | Behavior while a writer is present; see PI-08 |
| PI-08 | Design 2.11 concurrency contract (one writer and multiple readers) | Equivalence Partitioning | Use 16 frames. One thread inserts 2000 rows, each with content that is a deterministic function of `id`, and after each `Insert` completes adds the `Rid` to a list protected by a mutex. 4 threads repeatedly `Scan` the same table and call `Get` on the `Rid` values in the list. Run under ThreadSanitizer; `test_paged_database_one_writer_with_concurrent_readers` | Every row returned by a scan equals the content determined by its `id`, and a single scan contains no duplicate `Rid`. The row count of each scan is at least the number of inserts completed before the scan began. Every `Get` equals the expectation. At the end the scan returns 2000 rows, and TSan reports nothing | A consistent snapshot across pages; Design Document 2.11 states explicitly that there is none |
| PI-09 | Design 2.12; ADR-0029 | Equivalence Partitioning | Use 16 frames and tables H, F, G and B. H is 4 pages, F is 12 pages, G is 4 pages and B is 60 pages. Record the `misses` of the last scan of H; `test_paged_database_policy_switch_changes_misses_not_results` | The result of every scan, as a set of rows, is the same under the two policies. In the last scan of H, `misses` is 4 under `kLru`, which is the page count of H, and `kTwoQ` has 0 `misses` | Which policy is better under other access patterns |
| PI-10 | Design 2.15 durability | State Transition | (a) Insert rows, call only `Close`, then `Open` again. (b) Insert rows, skip `Close`, let the object leave scope, then `Open` again. (c) Call `Close` twice. (d) Call `Table` and `CreateTable` after `Close`; `test_paged_database_close_and_destructor_flush` | (a) and (b): the rows are present after the reopen, and (b) writes nothing to stderr. (c) The second call returns success. (d) The result is `kClosed` | A write-out failure in the destructor. A write failure cannot be injected into a real file, so it is not tested |
| PI-11 | Design 2.15 durability, I/O failure | Fault Injection | Open a `MemoryPageStore` with `OpenWithStore`, inject a sync failure, insert rows and `Close`. Call `Sync` on a `FilePageStore` over a real file; `test_paged_database_close_reports_sync_failure` | `Close` is `kIo`, and `Sync` on the real file returns success | Whether `F_FULLFSYNC` and `fsync` push the data out of the storage device, which cannot be observed inside the process |
| PI-12 | Design 2.1 dependency direction | Equivalence Partitioning | Scan the `#include` lines of `include/` and `src/`; `test_layering_paged_depends_only_on_domain_and_itself` | Files in `paged/` include only `domain/` and `paged/`. The byte layer (`tuple_codec`, `slotted_page`, `crc32c`) does not include `buffer_manager.h` or `page_store.h`. Files outside `paged/` do not include `paged/`, except tests and `bootstrap.cpp`. The scan finds at least 20 files in `paged/` | The standard library and system headers |
| PI-13 | Design 2.15 file size; Design 2.2 `IPageStore` | Boundary Value Analysis | Append 1024 zero bytes to a new database that was closed, `Open` it, create a table with 100 rows, `Close` and `Open` again. | PI-14 | Design 2.11 (opening a table); Design 2.15 "Damaged data at open"; 3.1 open steps 6 and 7 | Fault Injection | Create `a_table` with 100 rows of 218 bytes in a new file and `Close` it. | PI-15 | Design 2.10 free page and `free_list_head`; 3.1 open step 8; 2.15 "Damaged data at open"; ADR-0046 | Fault Injection, Error Guessing | Create a database with a released chain of 3 pages and `Close` it. In copies, rewrite `free_list_head` to a page past the end of the file and to a page of a live table, and rewrite a free page's `next_page_id` to 0, to itself and to a heap page, sealing the checksum each time, and `Open` each copy. `Open` a copy with `free_list_head` 0, which is also what a file written before the field existed holds; `test_paged_database_verifies_the_free_list_at_open` | The healthy file opens and reports 3 free pages through `FreePages`, and the copy with 0 opens with none. Each damaged copy is `kCorrupt`, and the message names the rule that failed: past the end of the file, `page_type 2 instead of 3` for a heap page and `page_type 1 instead of 3` for the file header, or `visits page` for a loop | A damaged page that nothing in the list reaches |
| PI-16 | Design 2.6 and 3.4 `ADD COLUMN` on paged; ADR-0045 | Equivalence Partitioning, State Transition | Create a table of 300 rows over 3 columns, an INT primary key and two TEXT columns, on about 13 pages. | PI-17 | Design 2.6 `ADD COLUMN` checks; 2.5 check 1; ADR-0045 | Boundary Value Analysis | In a table whose largest row is 4052 bytes, add an INT column, and in another whose largest row is 4053 bytes, add the same column. The second is `kTooLarge` and the third `kInvalidArgument` naming `column`, and after both failures the store has the same bytes and an unchanged free list, no pin is left, and the table of the second call keeps its row and its 2 columns | None |

The paged execution integration tests (XI) run `PagedEngine` over `Database` and `MemoryPageStore`, `QueryService` over both engines, `Bootstrap`, the `.engine` command and the cross-engine equivalence.

| Test ID | Claim | Technique | Procedure | Expected Result | Not Covered |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| XI-01 | Design Document 2.13, resource ownership (no pins); 3.2 | Boundary Value Analysis | Use `PagedEngine` plus a `Database` with 4 frames and 660 rows of data: visitors 60, stories 240 and reactions 360. The data takes more than 12 pages. Run on a single thread. Read `RowCounts()`, then run a scan with `SELECT *`, a three-table join, an aggregate, a sort, a `LIMIT` for early termination, a table that does not exist, and an evaluation error. The missing table is a `ScanNode` built by hand, because the Binder rejects the name. Check `PinnedFrameCount()` after each statement. Separately walk the same table directly with `TableHeap::Scan()`; `test_paged_engine_queries_with_minimum_pool_leave_no_pins` | `RowCounts()` returns visitors 60, stories 240 and reactions 360 in catalog order. Every result equals the hand-computed result. The row sequence of `SELECT *` equals the sequence of walking `TableHeap::Scan()` directly, which is page chain order plus slot order. `PinnedFrameCount()` is 0 after every statement, including after errors and early termination | Several threads at the same time (XI-11). The number of page reads when `TableHeap` is opened on the first use of each table |
| XI-02 | Design Document 2.13 "ApplyChangeSet" table; 3.3 | State Transition | Run `INSERT`, `DELETE` and then `INSERT` through `PagedEngine`; `test_paged_engine_writes_are_visible_and_reuse_slots` | A `SELECT` after each write sees the change. `AffectedRows` is correct. The `INSERT` after the delete does not increase the page count. `PinnedFrameCount()` is 0 | None |
| XI-03 | Design Document 2.13 "ApplyChangeSet" table (relocation by an update), cascade | Equivalence Partitioning | Update one row so that its TEXT grows until it no longer fits in the page, which is the case where `TableHeap::Update` returns `kNoSpace`. Then cascade to stories and reactions with `UPDATE visitors SET username` to a longer name; `test_paged_engine_update_relocates_rows_and_cascades` | The row moves to another `Rid`. The row content is correct and the row count is unchanged. The references in the three tables are consistent, and no row is duplicated or lost | The concrete value of the new `Rid`, which is not a contract |
| XI-04 | Design Document 2.15 "Statement atomicity" | Equivalence Partitioning | On a database that holds data, run one write that violates the primary key, one that violates a foreign key, a `DELETE` of a referenced row, one whose evaluation divides by 0, and one whose row is too long. Call `FlushAll` before and after each. Compare every page's bytes in `MemoryPageStore` and `BufferStats::page_writes`; `test_paged_engine_rejected_statement_changes_no_page` | The page bytes are the same before and after. The second `FlushAll` writes out no page. The error kinds are those of 2.5 and, for the division, 2.3 | Failure during apply (XI-05) |
| XI-05 | Design Document 2.13 `ApplyChangeSet` table and rollback invariants; 2.15 "Statement atomicity"; ADR-0038 | Fault Injection, Boundary Value Analysis | Use a pool of 4 frames and three tables that span several pages. Make the n-th `WritePage` fail with `InjectFailure(Operation::kWrite, n)`, with n increasing from 1 until the statement no longer fails. A write of the write-back after a successful apply also counts toward n. For each n run an `UPDATE` that cascades, a multi-row `INSERT` of 300 rows and a `DELETE` of 150 rows. Three deterministic scenarios apply a hand-made `MutationPlan` through `ApplyChangeSet` to pages that are full to the byte. Each is followed by a change for a table that does not exist, so that the apply fails after the earlier changes. The first has two rows that swap their sizes. The undo grows the first row back only after the second row shrank, and page allocation is made to fail so that a wrong order cannot recover by moving a row. The second is an `Update` whose page space was taken by the undo of a `Delete` in another page, so the undo falls back to `Delete` plus `Insert`. The third is an update that moved a row, which the undo reverses by deleting the moved copy. One more case: an `UPDATE` makes a row that no longer fits need relocation, and `InjectFailure(Operation::kAllocate, 1)` makes the allocation of the new page needed for the relocation fail, so `Delete` has succeeded and `Insert` fails; `test_paged_engine_apply_failure_rolls_back_at_every_write_position` | The error for every n is `kIo`. When the message does not end with `; statement was applied but not saved`, the failure came during the apply, and the contents of the three tables afterwards, taken as the multiset of `SELECT *`, equal those before the statement. When the message ends with it, the failure came during the write-back, and the contents equal those after the same statement run without a failure. Each of the three statements has at least one n of each kind. `PinnedFrameCount()` is 0. When n exceeds the number of writes, the statement succeeds. In each hand-made scenario the error is `kBind` with the message `unknown table: ghost`, without `rollback failed`, and the rows equal the rows before the change set. In the relocation case the old row that was deleted by `Delete` is restored, with the row count and content unchanged, and no page is added and no page is written | Failure of the undo (XI-06). The position of `Rid` |
| XI-06 | Design Document 2.13 "ApplyChangeSet" table (undo failure); 3.7 "Apply failure" row | Fault Injection | Run the cascading `UPDATE` of XI-05 on 4 visitors, 60 stories and 60 reactions, and for each write position also make read m = 1, 2, ... fail with `InjectFailure(Operation::kRead, m)`, so the read failure lands in the plan, the apply or the undo. Scan the combinations of (n, m); `test_paged_engine_rollback_failure_is_reported` | More than 20 combinations fire the read failure. Each returns `kIo` with a message that starts with `injected `. At least one combination produces `rollback failed`, with the message format `<original message>; rollback failed: injected ...`, and then at most one row differs from the state before the statement. A combination without that text leaves the state before the statement, or the state after it when the message ends with `; statement was applied but not saved`. `PinnedFrameCount()` is 0 for all combinations, and nothing crashes | The contents of the table after an undo failure. Design Document 2.13 says only "may contain partial changes" |
| XI-07 | Design Document 2.13; 2.15 "Damaged data at open" and "Damaged data after open" rows; 3.7 "Read error or corrupt page" row | Fault Injection | Rewrite one byte of one page of a table in `MemoryPageStore`, then read the row counts. Inject one read failure and query two tables. Inject a read failure in the middle of a scan of a table that is already open. Walk the stream of a `DatabaseTableReader` (`HeapRowStream`) with a read failure injected in the third read. Rewrite a page of a table after its heap was opened and the page left the pool; `test_paged_engine_storage_errors_map_to_io` | `RowCounts()` fails as a whole with `kIo` and a message that starts with `corrupt: `. The table that owns the corrupt page returns `kIo` with a message that starts with `corrupt: `, also when queried again. The read failure returns `kIo`, and the query on the other table succeeds. A failure in the middle of a scan is reported, and the next statement works. The stream returns the error once and no value on every later `Next`. `Scan` of an unknown table is `kBind` with the message `unknown table: ghost`, and a table name in capitals is found. A page that goes bad after the heap was opened gives `corrupt: ` on the next scan. No pin leaks | Triggering each `PageErrorCode`: XU-31 covers the mapping one by one, and XI-23 covers a broken chain |
| XI-08 | Design Document 2.6 (`CREATE TABLE` row); ADR-0042 | Equivalence Partitioning | Run a `CREATE TABLE` with `PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT`, `UNIQUE`, `DEFAULT` and `REFERENCES` through `QueryService`, then check `DescribeCatalog` and the constraints on stored data. Also try a table named `sys_x`; `test_paged_engine_ddl_scope` | The tag is `CREATE TABLE <name>`. The schema in the catalog contains all the constraints. `sys_x` returns `kUnsupported` and changes no page | The catalog after the file is reopened, which XI-10 and PI-03 check. `ALTER TABLE` and `DROP TABLE`, which XI-26 and XI-27 cover |
| XI-09 | Design Document 2.7 write rule | Equivalence Partitioning, Fault Injection | Import a CSV of 1000 rows with `ImportCsv`. The row count makes the data exceed one page. Open a second `Database` over a copy of the store bytes. Import a CSV of the same size again and make the third page allocation fail with `InjectFailure(Operation::kAllocate, 3)`. `CreateTable` allocates the first page and the root of the index, and the third allocation happens when a row does not fit in the first page. Import a CSV that contains one row longer than 4060 bytes; `test_paged_engine_import_table` | On success the tag is `IMPORT <table> (imported <n> rows)`, the row count is correct, and the second `Database` already sees all 1000 rows before any further statement. On an allocation failure the error is `kIo` with a message that ends with `; table <name> was created`, and the table exists and is empty. For an overlong row the error is `kUnsupported` with the message of 2.5, no page changes, and the table is not created, so it does not show in `.tables` | Other positions of failure during the import. The message of a failed cleanup, which the injection does not produce |
| XI-10 | Design Document 2.15 "Durability" row and 2.13 `kClosed`; ADR-0043 | State Transition | On a `FilePageStore` in a temporary directory, run `CREATE TABLE`, a multi-row `INSERT`, `UPDATE` and `DELETE`. Reopen once after calling `Close` and running a `SELECT` on the closed engine, and another time after only destroying the engine. After the reopen run an `INSERT` that omits the auto-increment column; `test_paged_engine_data_survives_close_and_reopen` | After a reopen by either method the data and catalog are the same. The `SELECT` after `Close` returns `kIo` with the message `database is closed`. Auto-increment continues from the maximum plus 1 | A real `kill` and a power loss. Design Document 2.15 gives no guarantee for a power loss |
| XI-11 | Design Document 2.14, the DB-level lock and the read paths of the two engines; ADR-0040 | State Transition | Run once per engine over `QueryService`. Paged uses 16 frames over an in-memory store, at least as many as the 8 concurrent readers (Design Document 2.13). Flatfile uses a temporary directory. The data is a table `t` of 240 rows that spans several pages and a table `u` of 60 rows that point at every fourth row of `t`. One writer thread runs 20 `UPDATE t SET v = v + 1` statements. 8 reader threads each run 40 iterations of `SELECT MIN(v), MAX(v) FROM t` and of a join query on `t` and `u`. The test also runs in a ThreadSanitizer build; `test_query_service_concurrent_readers_see_whole_statements` | The `MIN(v)` of every reader equals `MAX(v)`, so no reader sees a half-updated table. The values of each reader never go down and stay between 0 and 20. Every update affects 240 rows, and every query succeeds. After all threads finish, `MIN` and `MAX` are both 20, so no update was lost. `PinnedFrameCount()` is 0 on paged. The ThreadSanitizer build reports no data race, and all threads finish | Latency and throughput. Writer starvation. `buffer pool exhausted` when the frame count is lower than the number of concurrent readers, which depends on timing |
| XI-12 | Design Document 3.1; 2.2 `OpenPagedDatabase`; 2.15 "Reopening the same directory", "Unwritable directory" and "Damaged data at open" rows | Equivalence Partitioning, Fault Injection | Call `OpenPagedDatabase` in each of these situations. A directory that does not exist, whose parent directory exists. A path whose parent does not exist. An existing empty directory. A directory that contains `paged.db`. A paged database passed as `current` for the same directory, with a table created in its pool and not written out. A flatfile database passed as `current` for the same directory. A `paged.db` whose header was corrupted. A table whose page was corrupted. A directory with permission `0555`, also with a flatfile database opened in it as `current`, which holds no lock. A directory lock already held by `DirectoryLock::Acquire`; `test_bootstrap_paged_open_create_and_reuse_lock` | When the directory is created, `is_created` is true. `paged.db` and `.strata.lock` exist, and the lock has two owners, the `OpenedDatabase` and the engine. A missing parent returns `no such directory`. The row counts come from `RowCounts()` (`visitors=2 stories=0`). The same directory reuses `current->lock`, for a paged and for a flatfile `current`, and the lock outlives either owner. The new database sees the table that `current` had not written out. A corrupt header or table page returns a description string that starts with `corrupt: ` and returns no partial data. `0555` returns `directory is not writable`, also when `current` is a flatfile database without a lock. A held lock returns `database is locked by another process`. The order of lock release and write out: a single thread cannot observe the destruction order of `PagedEngine` and `OpenedDatabase`, and the member order in Design Document 2.1 guarantees it. The test checks only the two owners and the release after the last owner. The branch that aborts the open when `Flush` fails, because `Bootstrap` uses real files and a write failure cannot be injected |

### 3.3 Environment

Environment tests run `strata_cli` against a copy of `sample_my_db/`. Each test creates a working directory `work/`, copies `sample_my_db/` to `work/sample_my_db/`, replaces the `students.csv` of the copy with a version that has three more lines, checks that the four CSV files match their expected bytes, and runs `strata_cli` with `work/` as the current directory. The three added lines are line 12 (row 10 with `x1010` as its id), line 17 (row 15 with a semicolon in place of its last comma, which makes 4 fields where `students` has 5) and line 18 (row 15 with a sixth field). ET-19 and ET-32 to ET-36 do not use this copy, and ET-16 only scans source files.

Unless a row says otherwise, the copy keeps its `catalog.csv`, and the test sends `.open sample_my_db` first. The expected result then lists only what follows, because the two lines that `.open sample_my_db` prints (`loaded instructors=6 students=30 (rejected 3) courses=12 enrollments=144` and `students.csv: rejected lines 12, 17, 18`) are dropped before the comparison. Rows that send their own `.open`, or none, list the full output, and ET-26 compares the raw output. ET-18, ET-37 and ET-38 use a copy without the catalog. `<Tab>` stands for a tab character between columns.

The paged storage environment tests (PE) load the volume data (section 1) from memory into paged tables, with a buffer pool smaller than the data. They read no CSV file.

| Test ID | Claim | Technique | Procedure | Expected Result | Not Covered |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| PE-01 | Loading the volume tables into a pool smaller than the data (Design Document 2.6, 2.11, 2.12) | Equivalence Partitioning | In a new database, create the three tables of the volume data with `CreateTable`, with their columns and the primary key `id`, and insert all rows. Use 64 frames, which is 256 KiB and smaller than the data. Scan the three tables; `test_paged_environment_loads_data_with_small_pool` | Each of the three tables has 10000 rows. The scanned rows, sorted by `id`, equal the generated rows one by one. `evictions` is greater than 0 | None |
| PE-02 | Design 3.1; 2.12; ADR-0029 | Equivalence Partitioning | Build the same database as PE-01 in a new temporary directory. After `Close`, `Open` again with 64 frames, using `kLru` and then `kTwoQ`. Call `Table` on each of the three tables, call `ResetStats` and scan each table; `test_paged_environment_reopen_scans_match_the_rows` | The rows under both policies equal the generated rows. `page_reads` equals `misses`, because with read-only access every miss reads one page. `hits + misses` equals the total page count of the three tables, which comes from the number of entries in `FreeSpaceSnapshot` | None |
| PE-03 | Design 2.11 space reuse; ADR-0026 | Equivalence Partitioning | Build the same database as PE-01 in a new temporary directory. Record the page count of the store, delete 5000 rows of `parcels`, every other row, and insert the same 5000 rows again; `test_paged_environment_reuses_space_after_deletes` | The final page count exceeds the starting count by at most 2. The scanned set of rows equals the original `parcels`, and `CheckStructure` passes for every page of `parcels` | None |
| PE-04 | Design 2.11 page reuse; 2.6 `DROP TABLE` and `ADD COLUMN`; ADR-0046 | Equivalence Partitioning | Build the same database as PE-01 in a new temporary directory. Run `AddColumn` for an INT `DEFAULT 0` on `parcels` and record the page count, then `DropTable` on `scans`, create `scans` again and insert its rows; `test_paged_environment_alter_and_drop_reuse_pages` | After the `AddColumn`, `parcels` has the same rows as before with the new column 0 everywhere. After the drop and the new load, the page count exceeds the count after the `AddColumn` by at most 2, `depots` and `scans` equal their rows, and every page is a system page, a free page, a page of a live table or a page of its index. After a reopen `parcels` and `scans` equal their rows and the page check holds, and `CheckStructure` passes for every page of every table | None |
| PE-05 | Design 2.17; 2.16 height and size; ADR-0049 | Boundary Value Analysis | Build the three tables of PE-01 with each key also inserted into its index, then call `CheckIndex` and `Shape` for the three tables, look up 100 ids of each, close and open again; `test_paged_environment_volume_data_indexes` | Each table has an index of height 2 with between 35 and 70 leaves, as 10000 rows fill 35 to 70 leaves of 144 to 289 keys, the checks pass, every lookup returns its row, and the shape after the reopen equals the shape before | The file size and the fill factor, which are measured by hand, not asserted |
| XE-04 | Design 2.16 point lookup; ADR-0052 | Equivalence Partitioning | On the paged volume database run 50 point queries `WHERE id = k` on `parcels` for ids that exist, and the same 50 with `WHERE id + 0 = k`, which the index cannot serve, in a pool larger than the table, counting `FetchPage` calls and measuring the median time of each; `test_environment_point_lookup_with_and_without_an_index` | The results are equal. Each indexed query makes 3 `FetchPage` calls and each other query at least as many as `parcels` has pages. The medians are printed and no time is asserted | The interpretation of the times, the date and the environment, which this document does not record |

The paged execution environment tests (XE) load the volume data, as CSV files in a temporary directory, and `sample_my_db/` into both engines and compare the results. XE-03 prints the timings of both engines.

| Test ID | Claim | Technique | Procedure | Expected Result | Not Covered |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| XE-01 | Design Document 2.8; ADR-0042 (`.import` loads into both engines); 2.13 (a pool smaller than the data) | Equivalence Partitioning | Write the CSV files of the volume data to a temporary directory with `WriteScaleCsv`, and import `depots.csv` (`--pk id --type id=INT --skip-bad-rows`), `parcels.csv` and `scans.csv` into both engines with `ImportCsv`. Paged uses a pool of 64 frames, and the data exceeds 500 pages. Run 19 queries, which are 4 basic queries and 15 more join, aggregate, sort and `LIMIT` queries, and compare them according to 2.8; `test_environment_volume_data_queries_match_on_both_engines` | The two engines are equivalent on every import and every query. The paged result of the import of `depots.csv` is `IMPORT depots (imported 10000 rows, skipped 3)` with the three notes `skipped line 12: id 'x10' is not INT`, `skipped line 17: expected 3 fields, found 2` and `skipped line 18: expected 3 fields, found 4`, and the imports of `parcels.csv` and `scans.csv` report 10000 rows and no note. The `BufferStats` of paged show that eviction occurred, because `evictions` is greater than 0. The paged file has more than 500 pages. No pin leaks | Foreign keys and `UNIQUE`. `.import` does not infer these two, and constraint checks are covered by XI-18 and XI-20 |
| XE-02 | Design Document 2.8; 2.5 (constraint rules) | Equivalence Partitioning, State Transition | Load `sample_my_db/` into flatfile, then copy it into paged with 16 frames using `DescribeCatalog` and `SELECT *`, including foreign keys and `UNIQUE`. Run 47 queries and writes, including a four-table join, RESTRICT, a cascade through a table that the test adds, and an `ALTER TABLE ADD COLUMN` and a `DROP TABLE` of that table and of a referenced one, and compare them according to 2.8; `test_environment_sample_database_matches_on_both_engines` | Both engines hold the same four tables before the first statement. Every statement is equivalent. Every table is the same at the end. No pin leaks | None |
| XE-03 | Design Document 5.1 (timings of the two engines) | Equivalence Partitioning | Write the CSV files of the volume data to a temporary directory, and let each engine measure the `.import` load of the three files, as the median of 3 runs on fresh databases, and four query workloads, as the median of 5 runs each. The workloads are the four basic queries, one three-table join with `GROUP BY`, `LIMIT 10` behind `ORDER BY id`, and `LIMIT 10` without `ORDER BY`. The median of each item is printed; `test_environment_timing_of_both_engines` | The median in milliseconds of each engine and workload is printed. The results of the two engines are equivalent for every workload except `LIMIT 10` without `ORDER BY`. There the rows that come back are undefined, so the test checks only that each engine returns 10 rows and 2 columns. The test asserts no time | The interpretation of the values, the date and the environment, which this document does not record. The throughput of multiple threads |

The differential environment tests (DE) run `tests/differential/differential.py` against the built `strata_cli` and the `sqlite3` module of Python. They use neither the volume data nor `sample_my_db/`; the corpus creates its own tables, and the harness works in a temporary directory.

| Test ID | Claim | Technique | Procedure | Expected Result | Not Covered |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| DE-01 | Design 2.3 (differences from SQLite), 2.8 and 3.6 ("Compare with SQLite" row); 2.6 `ADD COLUMN` and `DROP TABLE` on paged; ADR-0047 | Equivalence Partitioning | Call `main` of the harness on a copy of the corpus with `STRATA_CLI_PATH`, which is what `python3 -I tests/differential/differential.py --cli build/strata_cli` runs; the corpus holds multi-table joins, aggregates, sorting, functions, writes, constraint errors, evaluation errors and bind errors, and the DDL sequences `ADD COLUMN` of each type, `DROP TABLE` of a referenced and an unreferenced table and a table created again under a dropped name; `test_end_to_end_corpus_agrees_with_sqlite` | The last line is `differential: <n> statements, <e> expected differences, 0 unexpected` and the exit status is 0, with `<n>` the number of statements and `<e>` the number of markers of the corpus. Flatfile and paged agree on every statement, each marked statement still differs from SQLite, and every one of the 14 areas of 2.3 is marked at least once | Statements outside the corpus, the order of rows that the corpus does not mark as total, and whether a marked statement differs in the way its area names (the harness compares only that the statement differs) |
| DE-02 | ADR-0047 (marker enforcement on the real corpus) | Error Guessing | Call `main` on two copies of the corpus, one with a `-- differs` marker deleted and one with a marker added to a statement that agrees; `test_end_to_end_markers_are_enforced` | Each run prints one `unexpected: line <n>: <statement>` report and exits with 1. The report of the deleted marker says what Strata and SQLite answered, and that of the added marker says `marked as differing (<area>)` | None |
| DE-03 | Design 3.6 comparison with SQLite; ADR-0047, ADR-0052 | Equivalence Partitioning | Add at least 12 statements to `corpus.sql` that read by key: `id = c` present and absent, `id >= a AND id < b`, `<` and `>` at the ends, `0` and a negative key, `id < 2.5`, after an update that moves rows, after a delete and re-insert of a key, after `ALTER TABLE ADD COLUMN`, and after `DROP TABLE` with the table created again; `test_end_to_end_corpus_covers_indexed_reads` | The test counts at least 12 such statements, none with a `-- differs` marker, and the run of DE-01 reports `0 unexpected` with the marked count unchanged at 23 | Statements that fail with an error before the key conjunct: flatfile differs from SQLite and from paged there, and the harness needs the engines to agree, so XI-32 covers them |
| DE-04 | Design 3.6 CTest row; ADR-0057 | Equivalence Partitioning | In a new build directory run `cmake -S . -B <dir> -DCMAKE_BUILD_TYPE=Release`, `cmake --build <dir> -j 8`, `ctest --test-dir <dir> -N` and `ctest --test-dir <dir> -R differential --output-on-failure`, then configure another directory with `-DCMAKE_DISABLE_FIND_PACKAGE_Python3=ON` and run `ctest -N`; no test function, a shell check | The first `-N` lists `differential`, `differential_unit` and `strata_tests`, the run ends with `100% tests passed, 0 tests failed out of 2` and no skipped test. The second configure prints `Python3 not found: the differential tests are not registered` and its `-N` lists only `strata_tests` | A machine whose only Python is older than 3.10, and the time of the run |
| DE-05 | Design 2.13, 2.16, 2.17, 3.8 to 3.10; ADR-0050, ADR-0053 | Equivalence Partitioning | For seeds 11, 12 and 13, generate a script of 3000 rows inserted in random key order and 300 operations (batch inserts, point and range deletes, updates that grow a row, a duplicate insert, key reads), run it on `flatfile` and on `paged` through `strata_cli` in new directories, and keep a Python model of the table; the paged run ends with `.dump index t`, `.open` of the same directory and `.dump index t` again; `test_index_scale_agrees_between_engines_and_with_a_model` | The output of `flatfile` equals the output of `paged` line for line, the final `COUNT`, `SUM(id)`, `SUM(grp)` and `SUM(LENGTH(v))` equal the model on both engines and after the reopen, and both index reports say `check: ok` with the model's key count, a height of at least 2 and more than one node | Index heights above 2 (the generated tables stay near 3000 rows); a table whose key is not the first column; concurrent use |
